# Performance implementation tracker

## PF17 — Performance overhaul ideas

**Completed safeguards follow-up:** [PSF01–PSF06](performance-safeguards.md) implement six targeted reductions in repeated validation and read/save preparation, with a [plain-language specification](../projects/completed/performance-safeguards-feature-spec.md) and [implementation design](../projects/completed/performance-safeguards-tech-design.md). Focused behavior checks and finite comparisons are complete; benefits are workload-specific and ordinary eligible-save cost remains inconclusive. The separate [optional-profiling package](profiling-controls.md) has finished code and focused checks, with both additional 60-second off/on observations recorded, but their materially different successful activity leaves its required matched comparison unqualified. Neither package extends that allowance or establishes smooth 3× gameplay.

**Implementation authorized October 16, 2026; in progress.** The earlier source-only assignment is superseded by Mike’s instruction to implement and check the candidates in batches, with at most five full non-compromise performance runs. The [plain-language project specification](../projects/performance-overhaul-feature-spec.md) contains the complete 23-idea inventory, priorities and gameplay tradeoffs; its [technical design](../projects/performance-overhaul-tech-design.md) gives source owners, order and dependencies. Read the project's [evidence audit and per-change evaluation instructions](../projects/performance-overhaul/evaluation.md) before selecting any implementation slice. Historical numbers are directional clues, not certified causal facts or promised gains.

This is the candidate/status owner. Existing PF/PS/PW owners retain their broader contracts; these cards refine and link that work rather than create duplicate implementation authority. Implement the agreed batch, while retaining only changes that pass their behavior and combined performance checks. Conditional architecture alternatives receive an explicit source-backed disposition; gameplay compromises follow the ordinary combined result if needed. Implementation is now authorized under the [batched execution plan](../projects/performance-overhaul/execution.md); full non-compromise performance runs are limited to five.

**Scope correction — October 17, 2026.** Mike explicitly required the entire implementation. Earlier checked cards sometimes meant a working first slice or a decision to defer an alternative; that overstated completion. The known partial cards below are reopened. Before editing a selected candidate, read the [implementation guide](../projects/performance-overhaul/implementation-guide.md) and its expanded card, including its continuation beyond the first prototype. Before the implementation-only direction below, checkboxes meant the agreed card implementation and required focused checks were complete; a limited implementation or a decision not to attempt the remaining mechanism does not qualify. Negative experiments and correctly conservative fallbacks remain useful evidence, but do not erase unfinished agreed scope. Smooth 3× acceptance remains separate and open.

**Implementation-only direction — October 17, 2026, 18:21 EDT.** Mike requests finishing every remaining implementation without more testing/profiling. The five previously partial cards below now have their selected code installed; their checkboxes record code completion under that direction, not executed runtime or performance acceptance. All 23 original ideas have an implemented or tried-and-rejected disposition, with PF17.5 explicitly rejected. Conservative unsupported cases remain named rather than silently changing gameplay. The final integrated TypeScript compile and source review pass after two integration corrections; smooth 3×, full-game retention and the separate save-continuation capacity checkbox remain open. The newer PSF/PC implementation packages are tracked separately from this original implementation inventory.

**Current implementation checkpoint:** Ordinary saving, numeric/status/work reads, entity storage, animal timers, local simulation/perception/material work, public updates and interface/thought preparation are integrated with focused evidence in the [implementation results](../verification/performance/overhaul-implementation.md). Held-index work, current account/checkpoint recovery and exact delayed-value save barriers pass their recorded checks. Follow-up review fixes include repeated same-edit entity lookups, exact current-value reuse, deadline-only animal reviews, selective static condition candidates, shared bounded history/canonical writes and coalesced text indexing. Conservative fallback remains where dependency coverage is incomplete; owner reports state its scope.

**Latest complete observation: 2.995× over 605.011 seconds, walking confirmation p95 1.111 seconds and maximum 3.870 seconds, idle-to-displayed movement p95 1.089 seconds, maximum drawing gap 1.592 seconds. Smooth 3× remains unmet: one working minute runs at 2.156× before catch-up.** This seventh compromise observation includes retained forecast-write savings and the repaired driver under the same twentyfold work durations/four-metre noticing policy. All 16 resident humans survive, work, sleep and wake, with 368 produced-food meals. Thirteen walks are blocked, none busy; four browser reads abort. A diagnostic write fails, and postmeasurement checkpoint preservation fails busy, leaving no usable saved continuation. New tracing directly identifies 3.204 seconds of the worst walk waiting for an earlier save. Source/bindings and exact owned cleanup pass. [Current result and limits](../verification/performance/overhaul-compromises.md#seventh-combined-compromise-result--catches-up-walking-and-drawing-still-fail).

**Current focused follow-through:** Complete walking-request tracing is implemented and passes actual HTTP/PostgreSQL ordering/refusal/no-replay checks. Combining independent record removals and combining memory insertion/bookkeeping are rejected and removed: fewer SQL calls did not demonstrate faster complete operations. Sharing up to four simulation intervals also fails its complete native cost comparison despite fewer publications, with more sight/spatial preparation. Keeping supported earned character calculations through forecast rebuilding is retained after exact fixed-schedule/reload checks and lower CPU in both unsampled candidates than both controls. The previous batch passes combined TypeScript/build/config checks. The seventh observation above measures that combination. A correct prepared-data handoff is then tested with four intervals and removed: both changed CPU samples exceed both controls with no clear elapsed gain. The ordinary one-interval policy remains. Optional-index priority and exact manual specimen capture pass focused PostgreSQL proofs. The priority comparison is complete: corresponding player commands finish sooner, but average total catch-up and process CPU rise. Priority is retained as an explicit responsiveness tradeoff. The complete accepted-batch worker is installed; its new runtime/failure/performance qualification is unrun under the implementation-only direction. [Focused evidence](../verification/performance/save-and-publication-followthrough.md).

All five ordinary full observations are complete. The correctly isolated baseline reaches 1.303×; the ten-minute combined run reaches 1.098× with late deterioration, and the final ordinary run reaches 1.346× after impossible zero-allowance thinking preparation is removed. No clean causal combined percentage gain or smooth-3× pass is established. Subsequent work implements the general journal for eligible families, narrowly supported stationary crafting and offscreen visual suspension; their complete capacity/retention gates remain open. Four detailed-model lifecycle checks are complete. Both the different busy-witness memory-loss trial and the original source-preserving routine-grouping trial were tested and removed/rejected for their recorded costs. The [current plain-language bottlenecks](../verification/performance/bottlenecks.md) rank the remaining costs and limits. The [reopened execution plan](../projects/performance-overhaul/execution.md#reopened-full-implementation--october-17-2026) records the remaining implementation stages without authorizing a sixth ordinary full-performance observation.

- [x] **PF16.119 — Determine whether walking already shares pending world-save work:** source-only assessment completed; no ordinary ground-walking gap is found and no implementation is selected. The walking action, latest unsent simulation progress and its durable recovery receipt already share one world transaction. Waiting commands defer the next periodic save, a successful command resets its cadence, and waiting durability requests reuse that commit. Deferral ends five real seconds after the last completed progress save even if a command remains queued; this preserves the current crash-loss bound but can put an older save before the command. Five seconds is not a database-completion guarantee. A prepared background snapshot has already released resident history and cannot simply be replaced before its exact baseline/diff/revision settles. Moving to a remembered place separately may flush changed personal evidence before its scoped SQL lookup; unrelated position/needs changes skip that barrier, and the measured ground-click driver does not use it. The66world-commit spans totaling36.8seconds overlap SQL/other waits and are not removable cost. No executable edit, check, database or game ran. Repeated floating-point additions do not generally equal one anchored multiplication, and effective writes increment revisions in their actual order; explicit status stepping also advances effects without changing the world clock. Exact deferral must retain enough information to reproduce actual integration batches and ordered writes, including restoration and forks; a bounded integration journal plus per-record progress is one candidate, not an accepted algorithm. Meter-only deferral also leaves the repeated status elapsed/work writes and coherent publication multiplier, so the complete scope crosses stages 4–6. That complexity and replay/retention cost did not justify beginning the broad representation change before the narrower measured owner corrections. - [x] **PF16.46 — Audit repeated durable publication across simultaneous resident intentions:** completed the preregistered read-only trace through the existing routine driver, mutation lane, service transition and transactional store; no runtime grouping was attempted. The diagnostic driver deliberately awaits each resident's initial durable job, real plan admission and terminal diagnostic publication before selecting another resident, so it does not supply a burst of already-queued resident transitions to amortize. Real transitions also load the current admitted job and validate current dependencies inside the mutation lane, then prepare/freeze/persist/install one snapshot before returning. The store can complete several changed response receipts in one world transaction, but its ordinary-command receipt and current authority fence each describe one operation. A generic batch would therefore require explicit per-operation fences/receipts, deterministic refusal isolation, final dependency checks, rollback/recovery and publication accounting across the service/store boundary; wrapping current calls in a transaction or retaining only the final caller's authority is insufficient. Existing independent PostgreSQL write grouping already amortizes record/history statements within one commit. Current PF16.26 attribution must establish whether the remaining separate commits justify PF07's broader coordinator; no grouping delay, early success, second writer or reduced durability was added. This closes the source/architecture audit, not PF07 or current full-game performance.

The architecture cross-check uses primary engine sources rather than transplanting a new manager: [Unity's update guidance](https://unity.com/how-to/advanced-programming-and-code-architecture) recommends avoiding inactive callbacks, [Factorio's shared radar registrations](https://www.factorio.com/blog/post/fff-421) illustrate eliminating overlapping repeated work, and [Unreal's significance system](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/significance-manager-in-unreal-engine) describes optional detail/frequency decisions. OpenLegend already has shared update and spatial owners, so these sources support refining actual dependency/consumer boundaries. They do not prove a bottleneck here or authorize dropping mechanics/evidence for less-visible characters. PF16.22 removes an unconsumed display reader; PF16.23 first audits proportional publication at its existing owners.

Batch 06’s [continued native/caller review](../verification/rivals-and-contested-ground.md#continued-native-collision-and-continuation-review--october-14-2026) shares carried-item naming once across actual offered shot descriptions and checks equivalent output through ordinary NPC choices, paged NPC consideration and public player attack details. Projectile boundary preparation prunes bodies outside conservative travel bounds before preparing their complete relevant motion, while an interior-turn and an irrelevant-body case retain exact contact, native outcomes and saved randomness. These scoped caller/mechanical comparisons do not establish sustained combat, population or broader PF capacity.

Refusal isolation, final sequence/event reconciliation, plan attempts and intermediate revisions prevent simply executing commands in place. Freezing only an accepted plain nested result before reopening it would be an approximately 6–12-line owner-local candidate, preserving isolation while trading a freeze for later snapshot copies; it removes no finalization and has no measured benefit. No freezing or in-place shortcut was attempted or selected after this attribution. A separately registered implementation trial would require stronger cost/allocation justification. Existing broader proportional-publication work remains under PF16.23/PF08/PF09.

## Order and stop rule

For the selected data-foundation work, qualify the [first/growth workload profiles](../../archive/07-technical-architecture/data-delivery-and-scale.md#1-what-scaling-means-for-this-product) and the [delegated performance direction](../performance.md#selected-approach). Tiny-world budgets below remain useful early checks; passing them alone does not complete the selected first-release target. PF11/D5 retain full player/network/browser qualification, and D6 retains distributed expansion. Replace the withdrawn 20 ms recall gate with measured end-to-end budgets and relevance evidence.

Run PF00 as a short baseline pass, then take the highest measured cost per unit of implementation effort. The initial order below reflects the known query amplification and polling, not a measured ranking of live latency. After each task, repeat the same relevant workload, report before/after percentiles and costs, and rerank. Once the small-world budgets pass with headroom, stop adding mechanisms; retain later tasks as gated scale work.

PF00's short attribution milestone unblocks the immediate fixes. Its larger percentile/soak qualification can follow those fixes; do not build a general benchmarking platform before removing the observed query amplification.

Dependencies require the relevant interface or measurement, not final certification of an entire stage. If PF03 attributes remaining CPU cost to cold history, bring PF08 forward; do not require the CPU budget to pass before removing its measured cause.

PF05 keeps prompt committed publication, with the measured 100-viewer workload now justifying a 50 ms coalescing window to avoid redundant projection. Its movement-cadence experiment still depends on PF03. Stable task IDs identify scope; the Order column controls initial execution priority.

| Order | Task                                          | Expected benefit                                                          | Effort / gate                                             |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0     | PF00: reproducible baseline                   | Identify where a click actually waits; prevent optimizing the wrong layer | Small; required before changes                            |
| 1     | PF01: compact history/commit SQL              | Remove the confirmed per-event/per-witness query amplification            | Small–medium; immediate                                   |
| 2     | PF02: triggered narration and due work        | Eliminate idle story transactions and repeated actor scheduling reads     | Small narrator change, medium scheduler change; immediate |
| 3     | PF05: prompt scoped publication               | Remove avoidable visual delay and history refresh traffic                 | Small–medium; immediate publication first                 |
| 4     | PF03: cheap native steps and appends          | Reduce main-thread blocking and cost that grows with history              | Medium; choose measured hotspot first                     |
| 5     | PF04: isolate optional database traffic       | Keep inspection/diagnostics/background activity out of the command queue  | Small–medium; only if contention remains after PF01/PF02  |
| 6     | PF06: bound and coalesce optional diagnostics | Prevent trace bursts, large serialization and shutdown backlogs           | Small–medium; batching depends on measured growth         |
| 7     | PF07: bounded command microbatches            | Amortize commits during bursts without delaying lone commands             | Medium; only if commit overhead still dominates bursts    |
| 8     | PF08: bounded hot state and checkpoints       | Prevent aging worlds from increasing every-step cost                      | Medium–large; long-session gate                           |
| 9     | PF09: population indexes and deadlines        | Replace world-wide scans with relevant actors/regions                     | Medium; population-profile gate                           |
| 10    | PF10: worker isolation and prediction         | Keep rendering/I/O responsive when necessary work remains expensive       | Medium–large; explicit CPU or network gate                |
| 11    | PF11: larger-world qualification              | Establish actual capacity and next partition boundary                     | Gated; follows the production-data scale track            |

## Acceptance budgets

These are initial engineering targets for a named desktop and healthy same-host PostgreSQL, not measured results or external promises. PF00 must record hardware, process/build mode, database version/topology, world data, workload and achieved simulation speed. D09 remains the product device decision. Report p50/p95/p99, counts and raw sampled timings; tiny samples do not establish tail percentiles.

| Metric                                                                     | Small-world target                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Click to local destination/pending feedback                                | p95 within one 60 Hz frame; no network dependency                                                                       |
| Native command received to durable result                                  | p95 ≤100 ms, p99 ≤200 ms, including admission queue and database wait                                                   |
| Durable commit to affected player view emission                            | p95 ≤16 ms, p99 ≤33 ms under normal output load                                                                         |
| Click to first confirmed action update on local setup                      | p95 ≤150 ms, p99 ≤250 ms; also measure first changed position separately from accepted action                           |
| Click to first changed position for an accepted, unobstructed walk         | p95 ≤200 ms, p99 ≤350 ms on the local setup; predicted pixels do not count as confirmation                              |
| Native tick CPU at 1×                                                      | p95 ≤8 ms, p99 ≤16 ms per 50 ms batch on both fresh and mature tiny worlds                                              |
| Main-thread simulation work at 8×                                          | No continuous slice >8 ms; sustain requested simulation speed without growing debt                                      |
| Excess timer lateness                                                      | p95 ≤10 ms, p99 ≤25 ms during ordinary play; record GC pauses separately                                                |
| Ordinary walk commit without checkpoint, invalidation or narration trigger | ≤9 SQL round trips including transaction boundaries; witness count adds rows, not statements within a chunk             |
| Idle Narrator                                                              | Zero periodic claim queries when no queued/due work exists                                                              |
| Background contention                                                      | Optional inspection/tracing adds ≤10 ms to native command p95 at the small-world load                                   |
| Long-session behavior                                                      | Pending queues and caches remain within declared caps; no linear per-step dependence on cold historical rows after PF08 |

An injected slow database may exceed normal latency budgets. Correct behavior is bounded queues, responsive pending/paused UI and truthful durability state. Do not pass by dropping events, lowering requested speed silently, disabling relevant background load or hiding confirmed latency behind prediction.

PF03 snapshot freezing and bounded catch-up are implemented; [recorded profiling](../verification/performance-investigation.md#mature-world-tick-profiling) includes mature-save before/after runtime measurements. The next priority is dense first-exposure work and extended qualification of PF08 with naturally aging worlds; synthetic cold-history growth is measured. PF04/PF07/PF10 remain gated on residual measured cost.

## Current 3× local scene follow-through

[Plan](../projects/three-times-scene-performance.md) · [Evidence](../verification/three-times-scene-performance.md). PF00/PF03/PF04/PF05/PF06/PF08/PF09/PF12.3–8: source-specific flushes, durable-snapshot maintenance checks, early spending preflight, profile caching, plain-SELECT read routing, joined publication fences, PostgreSQL batching, narrower cognitive wake dependencies, root-only snapshots and coalesced bounded diagnostics are implemented. Routine animal appearances now update current visibility and remain available to interests without creating individual stored sightings or automatically requesting reasoning; meaningful changes still create evidence. Continued merged-build work shares observer sight setup, reconciles each small memory batch in one PostgreSQL statement, skips impossible maintenance work and isolates advisory-read failures with a bounded backoff.

Acceptance remains open: the final 60-second PostgreSQL sample reached 2.998× with the requested visible populations at both endpoints and no storage error, but retained a 1.461-second cold gap and 367-ms warm maximum. Earlier PostgreSQL runs hit storage timeouts under severe host CPU/memory contention. Targeted memory reconciliation preserved identical stored results with 16→4 statements per 1,000 changed sources. Repeat the maintained full-server fixture on a quiet host, including funded local cognition, natural history growth and browser input. Under PF09/PF12.3, moving sources still cause affected observers to rescan visible membership, and cognition separately evaluates visibility. Prefer a shared dependency-valid visibility query/changed-target path over another unscoped cache. Ordinary known-ID reads and necessary current-format initialization/recovery scans need no blanket abstraction; this does not authorize legacy conversion paths.

The requested follow-up review separated the base-world sighting policy’s importance, urgency and reasoning choices, skipped unchanged feature scans, isolated optional diagnostic/profiler failures and removed redundant caption/scene updates. The matched 20-second PostgreSQL samples did not demonstrate an overall speedup: warm update p95/max was 66/92 ms before and 73/323 ms after. Preserve PF00/PF05/PF08/PF09 acceptance; [review evidence](../verification/performance/05-september-history.md#requested-implementation-review-september-27) records the narrower verified improvements and remaining database/queue work.

The [startup/scale/save continuation](../verification/performance/05-september-history.md#startup-scale-and-save-latency--september-27) implements shared committed scheduling reads, SQL-free coverage cache hits, narrower memory invalidation, atomic job updates, grouped record writes and scalar-only position/observer snapshots. PF01's alternating 21-observer create/update case now uses 2 statements instead of 42 with identical reload. Sight-episode bindings opt out of map ordinals: one subject leaving view no longer rewrites 3,360 unchanged bindings in the targeted case. PF04/PF08 shared-read and restore/rollback checks passed on SQLite/PostgreSQL. A shared-queue context leak was reproduced and fixed. The normal incompatible development world was explicitly reset by owner request; new-world startup succeeds.

The [eight-fire 1080p follow-up](../verification/eight-fire-three-times.md) profiles the real production UI and native PostgreSQL path with 20 NPCs, 21 animals and 100 other objects. Shared sprite-shadow submissions and generator-input asset reuse address measured browser driver overhead without changing simulation, save ordering or public scope. SW18.15 owns the sustained result and failed samples; this does not qualify funded cognition, long-history growth, the full release population or physical low-end hardware. The mercenary workload now has a specific physical-device matrix in [V3D11](3d-pixel-art.md#v3d11--performance-devices-and-accessibility); include it in PF00's existing 1,000-intention/delay cases and 30-minute mixed-load soak before claiming equivalent capacity. Keep achieved simulation speed, model/cloth cost, persistence waits and rendered/input tails separately attributed.

**Known remaining work and reasons:**

- **PF00/PF01/PF05:** removing map-ordinal amplification improved the matched 120-second run from 77/139/483 ms to 70/115/334 ms update gaps (p95/p99/max), sustaining 3.000× without errors. The remaining worst gap overlapped a 327 ms save with multiple SQL waits. Diagnostic `EXPLAIN` samples distinguished database execution from round-trip time but did not reproduce the former 367 ms single-statement tail. Continue database/host attribution under the existing injected-delay qualification. **2026-09-28:** [background saves](../save-and-load.md#background-and-synchronous-world-saves) for simulation progress are implemented ([project](../projects/ordered-async-saves.md)): they are written in snapshot order without holding the mutation queue, with the durable publication boundary unchanged; commands and AI results keep synchronous saves that hold the queue until written ([SB21](../limits/persistence.md#sb21)), and simulation progress that would evict not-yet-durable records is still saved synchronously ([SB20](../limits/persistence.md#sb20)). Correctness, failure and crash drills pass. Matched shared-host scene runs show the remaining queue time in the cognition scene comes from the synchronous saves of commands and AI results (including conversation updates): at 25/100 ms per statement both builds run at 0.08–0.17× ([evidence](../verification/ordered-async-saves.md#matched-scene-runs-pf00-delay-cases)). A stall-free scene under slow storage now needs those moved to background saves with durability-gated publication (PF05, open decision), fewer round trips per save would shorten both kinds ([PF15](#pf15--save-round-trip-batching)), and a quiet-host rerun is needed before acceptance.
- **PF05/HE05:** full-game 130% scale and its keyboard/reload checks remain unqualified after the earlier browser tool URL security rejection. SW18.15 subsequently exercises sustained production rendering and native commands at 1920×1080 using a local browser harness; that does not cover the separate 130% layout/input acceptance. CSS anchors the hidden radio input and excludes it from text-input styling; retain the remaining scale-specific checks.
- **PF08/PF11:** two minutes with fixture cognition do not establish long-session heap stability, live model quality or hosted capacity. The final 120-second endpoint retained 5,376 awareness entries and about 81 MB collected heap; run the already specified aging/soak qualification rather than extrapolating the short sample.
- **PF14:** the scheduling change below shared only unchanged scheduling facts and coverage counts. Request-wide hydration and fresh validation were subsequently implemented under [PF14](#pf14--shared-cognition-preparation-and-complete-path-cost), with conversation, perspective and history-edit dependencies checked separately from the scheduling cache.

- [x] Add tick wall/native-work/yield, persistence diff/encoding/transaction, CPU/heap, event-loop/GC, busy-callback and requested/accepted/excluded-clock diagnostics; provide an offline native CPU profiler.

- [ ] Reproduce walking, gather, eat, rest/stop, pause/resume, a mature-history view and a first encounter. Compare fresh and preserved mature saves; replay equivalent streams against the baseline and candidate build. Verify outcome codes so a rejected walk is not counted as a successful-action sample.

Use zero-cost provider fixtures and explicit empty credentials/zero spending. Provider-quality acceptance stays separate.
- [ ] Measure the asynchronous PostgreSQL adapter before population growth. Preserve immediate command durability and one world writer. Do not describe the adapter as distributed/scalable persistence; specialized repositories remain gated production work.
- [ ] Capture an initial short trace with at least 30 successful native commands and representative timer work to select the first bottleneck. Before final acceptance, run at least 1,000 native intentions per latency case after warm-up, a 30-minute mixed-load soak, and sampled database delay at 0/25/100 ms per operation. (2026-09-28: the scene profiler now injects per-statement delay and attributes each save to preparation, statement round trips and main-thread time, with a host probe; [shared-host attribution](../verification/ordered-async-saves.md#pf00-save-path-attribution-stage-1). Quiet-host percentiles, the 1,000-intention cases and the soak remain.) Include simulated provider delay, narration due/idle, diagnostics open/closed and periodic checkpoints. Use disposable databases and no paid calls. Keep cold-start/recovery measurements separate.

Attribution exit: stage timings, query count and a short reproducible baseline identify the first intervention. Full exit: larger before/after samples and soak results are recorded in Verification. Fixture/native measurements do not establish live model latency. Historical fixture failures retain their recorded revisions in TODO; inspect current source and check results before carrying a failure forward, and repair any still-affected fixture before using its assertions as evidence.

## PF05 — Public view and browser responsiveness

The [October 14 world-boundary review](../verification/world-engine-boundaries.md#requested-full-review--october-14-2026) shares exact installed implementation metadata only on fully immutable data and preserves the same ordered 1,978 complete-catalogue choices. It eliminates repeated warm installation-pin reads; overlapping whole-call timing ranges do not establish an end-to-end speedup. Mutable/draft data, support predicates, quantities and permissions remain fresh. Retain the existing full-catalogue inventory × target fan-out, structural-preview attribution and sustained latency/capacity qualification below and under AC11; no new stock cap, index or worker is introduced.

[AV01's second review](../verification/rewarding-expeditions.md#second-requested-review--october-7-2026) removes repeated identity hashing for unchanged immutable recipe definitions and fixes stale selected recipe details after another inspection. Its actual 24-publication specimen retains one fresh compilation per changed world and bounds the selected display-key cost through the real compact inventory. Record-page cost improves in that specimen; varied publication tails, complete-menu structural previews, browser/React attribution and wider capacity acceptance below remain open. If per-publication compilation becomes material, narrower reuse first needs an explicit compiler-dependency contract: the current trusted callback accepts the whole world. No cross-world compiled-validity cache, truncated choices or new content cap is introduced.
**AV05 second review, October 7:** gathering shortcuts now filter by actual offers, group sources once and select nearest eligible sources without per-type sorting or unused target lookups. [The isolated 1,000-source/20-material comparison](../verification/river-meal.md#second-requested-review--october-7-2026) preserves ordinary selection and withheld-stock semantics. Unchanged native draft definitions now reuse the existing immutable pin cache, with edit-sensitive snapshots and no mutable-value cache. Native record integrity adds bounded capture/result checks; complete catalogue timing must be interpreted with host contention and remains separate from production timer/browser/population qualification. AC11’s remaining atomic cooking-preview extraction must preserve these checks as well as quantity/reservation/work admission. No new persistent cache or worker was added.

**AV05 review contribution, October 6:** player menus/rebuilt fire views now share one exact ingredient assignment per unchanged read; NPC choices sort perceived fires once. Shortage/equivalent-lot pruning removes the demonstrated hundreds-of-milliseconds impossible-recipe search without losing admissible choices. The [river review evidence](../verification/river-meal.md#requested-implementation-review) retains all 155 diagnostic cooking offers and actual hold/release invalidation. Whole-menu/NPC timings varied across local runs; no stable whole-path speedup or scale capacity is claimed. Remaining work is unequal-quantity assignment growth, safely extracting the atomic cooking-preview preflight under [AC11](action-capabilities.md#ac11--usability-and-measured-efficiency), and larger recipe/fire/actor measurements through the production server timer and browser. These diagnostics do not close PF05.

**PG03 measured catalogue follow-up, October 4:** request-scoped indexes and once-per-read tool/ammunition selection preserve previous native choices/facts without world effects. A 111-possession fixture exposes 1,405 complete choices, taking roughly 0.3–0.4 seconds in that sample. The software-browser 1,443-choice specimen varied from roughly 1.4–6.2 seconds to ready, with concurrency in the slower final-stage specimen. Initial rendering is 40 choices, explicit Show more adds 40, and collapsed detail bodies are deferred; search still covers the complete result. Client target/search filtering also reuses unchanged visible identities across position-only patches. That evidence demonstrates bounded initial DOM work, not an end-to-end latency improvement. [PG03 evidence](../verification/player-clarity-ui.md#requested-review-follow-up--october-4-2026) and [AP01](../limits/interface.md#ap01--ordinary-action-discovery) retain the historical measured scope; the current PG04 comparison follows below.

The second PG03 review prunes unrelated inventory candidates when browsing one selected item. The [October 6 expedition review](../verification/rewarding-expeditions.md#requested-review--october-6-2026) now shares exact successful method validation and carried-material totals on immutable world snapshots through their existing read owners. Sixteen records fall from 32 method compilations per page to one first-read compilation and none on repeats; with 1,023 carried lots, the matched warm page median is 27.53 → 2.09 ms. Crafting and physical-record projections use the same totals; mutable execution still checks current stock and meaning. Complete-catalogue inventory projection still repeats item detail/comparison and structural drop/split/accepted-offer previews; candidate menu samples vary enough that reliable full-menu latency improvement is not established. Attribute and reduce those remaining previews, including actor-inventory × local-target fan-out, before claiming complete-menu or wider capacity improvement. No eligibility cache, choice truncation or new stock limit is introduced.

[PG04](parallel-batch-03-personal-game.md#pg04--cheaper-action-availability) is delivered for the selected repeated preview cost: offer refusals share read-only admission and return before a draft, while drop previews omit discarded action-history construction and retain structural transfer checks. [October 4 evidence](../verification/command-frame-spikes.md#pg04-preview-work-and-larger-load-follow-up--october-4-2026) shows identical complete choices and native transitions, with a matched 1,443-choice warm native median of 272.42 → 175.87 ms. Actual production-browser catalogue reads include 115/1,443/5,291 choices; the final largest sample still takes 1.36 seconds to open and a 1.28-second median to refresh. No stored-inventory or discovery cap was added.

Bounded 60-second server and 1920×1080 Metal browser samples with 21 people, 21 animals and 100 objects sustain 2.99×, with all 59/52 commands successful respectively. Actual browser click-to-receipt p95 is 428.2 ms; frame-interval p95/p99/max is 16.6/17.5/59.0 ms. These are local diagnostics, not a matched whole-game speedup or uninterrupted-60-Hz acceptance. Queue/native/projection/commit spans are recorded separately; statement round trips remain most of the measured commit tail. The original eight-minute/eight-fire scene, older histories, genuine multiple players, physical lower-end hardware and reliable complete-path tail budgets remain unqualified. General SQL batching stays with PF15 and durable/background-save choices with their existing owners; no new optimization is inferred from overlapping wall spans.

[PG04](parallel-batch-03-personal-game.md#pg04--cheaper-action-availability) delivers scoped preview reuse: current profiling confirms the cost, and eight scheduled families now reuse the actual read-only prerequisite owner; the [October 4 extension](../verification/command-frame-spikes.md#pg04--meter-refilling-and-following-october-4-2026) qualifies replenishment/following separately on the existing clockwork demonstration. [Evidence](../verification/command-frame-spikes.md#pg04--pure-prerequisite-previews-october-3-2026) records equivalent results and about 65% cheaper strike previews per call in the matched full-game sample. Command/menu p95 and smooth frames remain unqualified; this does not close PF05 or select general SQL/scheduler changes. Delivered geometry work is not reopened.

### A further 90% end-to-end reduction

Treat this as an attribution target, not a result of multiplying microbenchmark speedups. PF00 must separate queue/native/commit/projection/SSE/render spans on the same scenario. Reducing one fraction of latency by 90% cannot reduce the whole by 90% unless it dominates. Use lazy spatial work and participant reuse already delivered; next target the measured dominant remaining layer through PF01/PF05/PF08/PF09 and EPR02/EPR03 rather than adding another cache speculatively. Keep first acquisition, dense no-route search, mature history, ordinary movement and full-stack sustained load separate. Do not change visibility, auditory evidence, durability or simulation time to meet a number.

Implemented and locally qualified on 2026-09-27 as a follow-up to [NC14–NC17](narration-and-conversations.md#compaction-delivery-and-evidence), implementing the consistent preparation boundary already tracked in [CR03/CR04](cognition-redesign.md#cr03--compact-english-context). Depends on the delivered conversation owner and PF00 attribution tools, not completion of broader population acceptance. The [compaction microbenchmark](../verification/conversation-compaction.md#conversation-compaction-review) measures seven preparation reads plus four metadata validations; it excludes surrounding cognition work and is not a complete-request SQL budget. That inspection identified repeated scope reads, overlapping recall/conversation hydration, and covered speech discarded only after retrieval/rendering. The [implementation plan](../projects/completed/postgresql-cognition-preparation.md) and [matched complete-path evidence](../verification/postgresql-cognition-preparation.md#postgresql-only-storage-and-shared-preparation) now cover request-owned scope/body reuse, early optional exclusion and shared fresh validation. Native race/lifecycle checks preserve required evidence and privacy. The 350-turn warm caller hydrates 65 bodies instead of 367; conversation metadata still needs fresh reads, and broader hosted/soak/model-quality acceptance remains separate.

- [x] **PF14.1 — Attribute the complete path.** Trace actual `AiDirector` and reflection consumers from admitted trigger through preparation, attention/routing, action preparation, generation and publication. Separate local CPU, database/lane wait, scheduling and provider wait; count executed SQL including transaction boundaries, hydrated rows/bytes, repeated rendering, prompt bytes and compactor calls. Start with existing bounded diagnostics and zero-cost providers, without a new profiling framework.
- [x] **PF14.2 — Share request preparation.** Reuse one actor/generation scope and source snapshot across trigger evidence, recall and conversation projection. Retain admitted hydrated records with source revisions and fetch only missing bodies. Exclude conversation-covered speech from optional recall before hydration/rendering, including duplicate groups, while preserving independently required triggers, commitments and corrections. Bounded optional recall is not the complete conversation; retain exact conversation membership and byte admission. Keep compaction policy in its existing owner and storage permission checks in the repository.
- [x] **PF14.3 — Avoid redundant freshness work.** Share validation of overlapping sources and skip repeat reads only when revision tracking covers every relevant dependency: restore generation, conversation membership, source edits/deletions, corrections/forgetting and perspective. Preserve bounded race handling, ordinary-append tolerance, checks after asynchronous work and atomic publication. Do not substitute actor-local revisions for global/event dependencies they do not track.
- [x] **PF14.4 — Qualify the result and remaining growth.** Compare the complete path before/after on the same PostgreSQL inputs: short conversations, warm summaries, cold/restore rebuilds, histories exceeding optional recall capacity, concurrent appends/edits and increasing retained history. Report sampled latency distributions, allocations/heap, SQL and hydration costs, prompt bytes and unchanged admitted outcomes; keep provider latency/quality separate. Include repeated compaction, cache invalidation/rebuild frequency, derived-row growth and explicit cold-limit failures under [LA236](../limits/narration.md#la236). Attribute remaining metadata scan/planner cost before choosing indexes or incremental validation; do not raise limits or add queues/caches merely to improve a microbenchmark.

Exit: duplicated preparation and unchanged-source validation are reduced with matched complete-path evidence, preserved privacy/evidence/budget semantics and passing native race/lifecycle scenarios. Record any remaining dominant cost and measurement limits. [NC12](narration-and-conversations.md#nc12--remaining-compaction-acceptance) retains behavioral and regression acceptance; PF11/D5 retain hosted/population qualification.

Local qualification limits: samples cover 5/150/350-turn inputs, ten repeated warm replies, restore and a 1,300-turn explicit cold failure. Summary storage remains one row per actor/conversation in the repeated run. Process CPU, SQL, hydrated bytes and sampled heap are recorded; allocation totals, peak heap, stable long-session tails and hosted contention are not established. Those broader PF00/PF11/NC12 gates remain open.

## PF15 — Save round-trip batching

**October 14 authorization:** Mike's renewed profiling/implementation request authorizes the measured batching trial and justified performance changes under PF16.7. The original unimplemented proposal and acceptance below remain historical design context; current measurements determine which mechanism is retained. No approval is inferred from the earlier proposal itself.

**Proposed 2026-09-29; not approved for implementation.** Question: should a save's SQL statements reach PostgreSQL in a few round trips instead of one per statement? The September 29 planning baseline measured 17–23 round trips per save on average (44 at most); do not treat that older measurement as a fixed count for every current save. Re-profile the current writers and actual database round-trip time before selecting a group, since synchronous saves hold mutation ownership while their transaction is written. Behavior and acceptance: [feature spec](../projects/save-round-trip-batching-feature-spec.md). Mechanism, options and limits: [technical design](../projects/save-round-trip-batching-tech-design.md). A 2026-09-27 multi-statement text attempt was rejected ([runtime performance](../performance.md)); this proposal keeps bound parameters and is gated on real round-trip time.

Depends on: PF00 profiler attribution (delivered). Coordinate with the current [D1/D2 history-storage owner](production-data.md#remaining-d1d2-implementation-and-evidence), whose integrated implementation also owns `world-records.ts`; the old branch name is not an outstanding merge dependency. Independent of PF05 (moving commands and AI results to background saves); the two combine.

- [ ] **PF15.1 — Decision gate and trial.** Measure the round-trip time of the database the game will actually use. Classify every current statement by result checks, visibility/constraint dependencies, conflict precedence and whether its effects are independent. On a disposable database, try combined statements (data-modifying CTEs): parent and child rows in one statement, re-parenting before a delete, new and changed rows of one table together, the same row twice, a failed check, a unique violation, the statement time limit on a group, parse/plan time of a 64-statement group, and actual transport round trips and save times at 0/5/25 ms delay per round trip. Prove bounded enqueue/flush progress, rollback before flush, invalid/expired scope refusal and no leaked work; do not rely on CTE listing order. Record results and the go/no-go decision; include the pipelining-driver option only if a driver change is on the table.
- [ ] **PF15.2 — Start and time limit in one round trip.** Send `BEGIN` and `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` together for write transactions.
- [ ] **PF15.3 — Adapter write groups.** Explicit enqueue admission that cannot await an unreachable future flush, bounded accumulation, active writable scope checks, flush before reads and `COMMIT`, semantic dependency barriers, checked results, round-trip metrics and the equivalent off path, as designed.
- [ ] **PF15.4 — Save writers use groups.** Preserve checked revision admission and failure precedence; group only proven-independent history, memory-source and record writes. Retain all constraint/visibility and write-before-delete barriers; memory index upkeep stays standalone after its required flush.
- [ ] **PF15.5 — Rest of the transaction (conditional).** Memory index upkeep and other write transactions, only if PF15.4 measurements show they matter.
- [ ] **PF15.6 — Qualification and default.** Equivalence with the setting on and off (row hashes, durable revisions), the failure and crash drills plus in-group failures, matched ABBA scene runs at 0/25/100 ms injected per actual round trip and on a quiet host, reporting logical statements separately; update LA174/LA175, [save/load](../save-and-load.md#background-and-synchronous-world-saves) and [Architecture](../architecture.md); decide the default.

Exit: a typical background save and command save use at most 4 round trips; database contents are identical with the setting on and off; failure outcomes match today's drills; the 25 ms PF00 case shows at least a 40% shorter median synchronous save in matched runs; the default is decided and documented. If PF15.1 shows the target database's round trips are too cheap to matter, record that and close PF15 without implementing.

## PG05 — Encounter delivery measurements

[October 4 encounter evidence](../verification/first-threat-encounter.md#performance-and-costs) preserves a short complete-server starter run at 1.003× normal speed (10 durable commands, observed 24–50 ms), without a long-session or lower-end claim. Matched 847-root crowded native scenes with and without this one controller both achieved about 0.27×; PF03/PF08/PF09/PF11 retain finalization, sensory/event fan-out, growing history and population qualification. No lossy sensing cap, dropped evidence, new cleanup horizon or speculative combat scheduler was introduced to disguise that failure. Death with 2,006 lots and million-unit quantities measured about 95–205 ms in ad-hoc runs; quantity does not expand the lottery. These are distinct workloads and not a before/after optimization claim.

## Future improvements from the foundation review

The [October review](../verification/foundation-review.md) adds no population or graphical capacity measurement. Current correctness fixes belong to its [immediate tracker](foundation-review-todos.md); retain the PF order/stop rule and existing whole-path acceptance.

[NET05/NET11](bounded-network-requests.md) adds shared finite-wait and unsettled-capacity guarantees to the existing coalesced operator reads below. FRL02 remains delivered within its recorded scope: one pending refresh and stale-result rejection do not establish that an ignored cancellation settles the underlying request. NET preserves completion-based polling and scope fences, and qualifies repeated timeouts without accumulating abandoned work; broader responsiveness and hosted capacity remain PF05/PF06/PF11.

- [x] **FRL02 — Bound optional operator reads by completion.** Implemented in the [imported-interface review](../verification/ui-implementation-review.md#follow-up-correctness-and-performance-review). Operations shares one in-flight refresh; its API rejects obsolete access before pruning checkpoint recovery records or adopting shared access. Diagnostics list/activity reads share one in-flight owner and one pending explicit refresh, with the next poll scheduled after completion. Page/filter/access changes, detail selection, Hide and unmount abort obsolete client reads. The native-backed Operations observation holds one read across the five-second cadence without accumulating requests. The follow-up controlled Diagnostics observation makes 11 requests, holds one for 6.6 seconds across two nominal intervals, and observes at most one active browser fetch with coalesced Refresh and no scheduling after Hide/unmount. Its fresh access page appears before the abandoned interception callback is released. Two fetches are aborted; retained interception callbacks briefly overlap, so this does not qualify cancellation of backend/SQL work. Controlled API checks preserve newer access and checkpoint records against an older reply. No new fetching framework, nominal poll interval or population-capacity claim is introduced. Sustained remote latency, server work and deployment qualification remain with PF05/PF06 and multiplayer operations.

- [ ] **FRL03 — Measure complete sensory/decision preparation before another cache.** As PF09/PF12/EPR02 work, vary distant roots while holding nearby permitted objects fixed, then vary local density independently. Attribute `event-audience.ts` receiver-roster rebuilding, `entity-references.ts` per-observer whole-entity hashing, and `ActorWork.refresh/ready` population scans/sorting through actual command/event/decision callers. Reuse existing counters, immutable inputs and semantic change metadata. If preparation dominates, reuse participant descriptors within proved unchanged phases or narrow preparation to permitted candidates, preserving exact identity/collision handling, receiver order, event-time positions, complete evidence, membership changes and restore. Measure cold rebuild, warm reuse, invalidation and complete server latency/allocations; no arbitrary audience/action cap, guessed TTL or separate scheduler. [The existing context-reference task](cognition-redesign.md#exact-entity-context-references) owns label preparation; this item supplies the coordinated measurement rather than a duplicate implementation task.

PF08/PF11/PF14 retain aged-history and hosted qualification. Continue separating NPC count, local density, inventory/history and simulation speed; a faster helper or bounded returned list does not establish bounded total preparation. The EPR05 durable-input prerequisite is delivered under [FR13](foundation-review-todos.md#fr13--complete-the-existing-durable-cognition-prerequisite), with held-save/failure/restore and PostgreSQL-reopen checks recorded in EPR05. Preserve that boundary while pursuing these separate performance improvements; broader cognition and population acceptance remains open.
